## GHSA-gj9m-f8ph-pmhr · gpustack

2026-11-09 · Python · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The application implicitly trusted requests originating from 'localhost' (127.0.0.1) by default, allowing unauthenticated access to an admin user if the `force_auth_localhost` setting was not explicitly enabled. This trust could be bypassed by an attacker manipulating HTTP headers like `X-GPUStack-Real-IP` or by leveraging a reverse proxy to spoof the source IP as localhost.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could gain full administrative access to the GPUStack instance, leading to complete compromise of the system.

### Key diff

```
--- a/gpustack/api/auth.py
+++ b/gpustack/api/auth.py
@@ -120,9 +120,6 @@ async def get_current_user(
             if token is not None:
                 user, api_key = await get_user_from_api_token(session, token)
 
-        if user is None and client_ip_getter(request=request) == "127.0.0.1":
-            if not server_config.force_auth_localhost:
-                user = await User.first_by_field(session, "is_admin", True)
         if user:
             if not user.is_active:
                 raise UnauthorizedException(message="User account is deactivated")
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the `force_auth_localhost` configuration option and the associated logic. It now unconditionally requires authentication for all requests, including those originating from localhost, thereby eliminating the implicit trust vulnerability.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 93 occurrences**

## GHSA-rm8c-2cv8-jwf6 · github.com/pocket-id/pocket-id/backend

2026-11-09 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.6 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the email verification token remained valid even after a user changed their email address. This allowed an attacker to initiate an email change, then revert the email address to the original, and still use the token sent to the original address to verify an arbitrary email address they controlled.

### Impact

An attacker could spoof a verified email address for any user, potentially gaining unauthorized access or control over accounts by associating an arbitrary email with a user's profile.

### Key diff

```
The provided diff is for a new file and does not show a specific code change that fixes the vulnerability. It introduces the email module, but the fix itself would be in the logic handling token validation and email address changes.
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff does not contain the actual fix for the vulnerability. It appears to be a new file `backend/internal/email/module.go` which defines the email sending module. The fix for the reported vulnerability would involve invalidating existing email verification tokens when a user's email address is changed, or ensuring tokens are tied to the current email address.

## GHSA-9qw7-j9xw-fv9c · isomorphic-git

2026-11-09 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The `isomorphic-git` library did not adequately sanitize file paths during checkout operations. Specifically, it failed to prevent path traversal using directory symlinks and did not properly validate special file names like '.' and '..' or '.git' and its short aliases. This allowed a malicious Git repository to define file paths that, when checked out, would write files outside the intended working directory by traversing through symlinked parent directories or by using specially crafted names.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious Git repository that, when cloned or checked out by a victim, would write arbitrary files to locations outside the repository's working directory. This could lead to remote code execution (RCE) if the attacker writes to sensitive system locations (e.g., startup scripts, configuration files) or overwrites critical application files.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/models/GitTree.js
+++ b/src/models/GitTree.js
@@ -45,8 +45,25 @@ function parseBuffer(buffer) {
     const type = mode2type(mode)
     const path = buffer.slice(space + 1, nullchar).toString('utf8')
 
-    // Prevent malicious git repos from writing to "..\foo" on clone etc
-    if (path.includes('\\') || path.includes('/')) {
+    // Reject reserved entry names, matching git's verify_path(): path separators,
+    // "." and ".." (which resolve outside the working directory), and ".git" (which git
+    // disallows as a path component). Checks mirror git's is_ntfs_dotgit() and
+    // is_hfs_dotgit(): case-insensitive, trailing dots/spaces stripped (NTFS),
+    // NTFS 8.3 short name aliases (git~1..git~9), and HFS+ ignorable Unicode
+    // characters stripped (zero-width joiners, directional marks, BOM, etc.).
+    const hfsClean = path.replace(
+      /[\u200C-\u200F\u202A-\u202E\u206A-\u206F\uFEFF]/g,
+      ''
+    )
+    const normalized = hfsClean.toLowerCase().replace(/[. ]+$/, '')
+    if (
+      path.includes('\\') ||
+      path.includes('/') ||
+      hfsClean === '.' ||
+      hfsClean === '..' ||
+      normalized === '.git' ||
+      /^\.?git~[1-9]$/.test(normalized)
+    ) {
       throw new UnsafeFilepathError(path)
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces stricter validation for file paths. It now explicitly rejects paths containing directory separators ('\' or '/') and special names like '.', '..', '.git', and NTFS 8.3 short name aliases for '.git'. Additionally, a new function `assertNoSymlinkInLeadingPath` was added to `checkout.js` to recursively check and prevent writing files through any symlinked parent directory components, ensuring files are only written within the intended working tree.

## GHSA-j8j4-rw65-56r6 · github.com/telepresenceio/telepresence

2026-11-09 · Go · **HIGH** 8.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The Telepresence Traffic Manager's gRPC interface lacked proper authentication. Any pod within the cluster could claim an arbitrary namespace and intercept services, as there was no mechanism to verify the identity or authorization of the connecting agent.

### Impact

An attacker could intercept traffic for any service, potentially gaining access to sensitive environment secrets and effectively performing a man-in-the-middle attack on internal cluster communications.

### Key diff

```
-	sessionID, err := s.state.AddAgent(ctx, agent, time.Now())
+	principal := verifiedAgentPrincipal(ctx, agent)
+	sessionID, err := s.state.AddAgent(ctx, agent, principal, time.Now())
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `managerTokenCredentials` mechanism for agents to send their ServiceAccount token with gRPC requests. The manager now verifies this token, ensuring that the connecting agent's identity (pod name, UID, and namespace) matches the claims in the bound token, preventing unauthorized agents from claiming arbitrary sessions.

## GHSA-6j3h-rp73-6rvf · github.com/telepresenceio/telepresence/v2

2026-11-09 · Go · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `SESSION_FIXATION→HIJACK`

### Root cause

The Telepresence traffic-manager allowed clients to supply an arbitrary session ID during the 'ReconnectClient' operation without verifying ownership. This meant an attacker could claim an existing session ID if they knew it, effectively hijacking an active client's session.

### Impact

An attacker could hijack an active client's session, potentially gaining unauthorized access to resources or impersonating the legitimate client within the Telepresence system.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cmd/traffic/cmd/manager/service.go
+++ b/cmd/traffic/cmd/manager/service.go
@@ -226,7 +226,7 @@ func (s *service) ArriveAsClient(ctx context.Context, client *rpc.ClientInfo) (*
 	SetGauge(ctx, s.state.GetConnectActiveStatus(), client.Name, client.InstallId, nil, 1)
 
 	return &rpc.SessionInfo{
-		SessionId:        string(s.state.AddClient(client, time.Now())),
+		SessionId:        string(s.state.AddClient(client, auth.PrincipalFrom(ctx), time.Now())),
 		ManagerInstallId: s.clusterInfo.ID(),
 		InstallId:        &installId,
 	}, nil
@@ -235,7 +235,10 @@ func (s *service) ReconnectClient(ctx context.Context, info *rpc.ReconnectClient
 func (s *service) ReconnectClient(ctx context.Context, info *rpc.ReconnectClientRequest) (*empty.Empty, error) {
 	ctx = managerutil.WithSessionInfo(ctx, info.Session)
 	sessionID := tunnel.SessionID(info.GetSession().GetSessionId())
-	if s.state.GetClient(sessionID) != nil {
+	if session := s.state.GetClient(sessionID); session != nil {
+		if err := state.ClientOwnershipError(ctx, sessionID, session); err != nil {
+			return nil, err
+		}
 		// We already know this client, so we don't need to do anything.
 		return &empty.Empty{}, nil
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `manager_token.go` file to handle manager token sources and credentials, ensuring that client authentication is properly established. Crucially, it modifies the `ArriveAsClient` and `ReconnectClient` methods in `service.go` to include principal (owner) information when adding or restoring a client session, and adds an ownership check (`state.ClientOwnershipError`) during `ReconnectClient` to prevent unauthorized session claims.

## GHSA-rr8v-fxxf-c986 · com.gradleup.shadow:com.gradleup.shadow.gradle.plugin

2026-11-09 · Java · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The application was creating ZipEntry objects using user-controlled names without proper validation. This allowed an attacker to include path traversal sequences (e.g., '..') in the entry names, which would then be used to construct file paths outside the intended directory during extraction.

### Impact

An attacker could write arbitrary files to arbitrary locations on the file system where the application has write permissions, potentially leading to remote code execution or data corruption.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/main/kotlin/com/github/jengelman/gradle/plugins/shadow/internal/Utils.kt
+++ b/src/main/kotlin/com/github/jengelman/gradle/plugins/shadow/internal/Utils.kt
@@ -32,8 +33,12 @@ internal inline fun zipEntry(
   preserveLastModified: Boolean = true,
   lastModified: Long = -1,
   block: ZipEntry.() -> Unit = {},
-): ZipEntry =
-  ZipEntry(name).apply {
+): ZipEntry {
+  if (name.split('/', '\').any { it == ".." }) {
+    throw GradleException("Malicious ZIP entry containing path traversal sequence: $name")
+  }
+
+  return ZipEntry(name).apply {
     if (preserveLastModified) {
       if (lastModified >= 0) {
         time = lastModified
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check within the `zipEntry` function to explicitly disallow path traversal sequences ('..' or '..\') in the entry names. If such a sequence is detected, a `GradleException` is thrown, preventing the creation of a malicious ZipEntry.

## GHSA-q4xm-p75h-h9p3 · github.com/pocket-id/pocket-id/backend

2026-11-09 · Go · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The OIDC service's client credentials grant implementation allowed clients to request tokens for arbitrary audiences. This meant that a client could obtain an access token for any audience, even if it was not explicitly authorized to do so, by simply specifying the desired audience in the token request.

### Impact

An attacker could use a legitimate client's credentials to obtain an access token for a different, potentially more privileged, service or resource. This could lead to unauthorized access to sensitive data or functionality within the system.

### Key diff

```
-	"github.com/pocket-id/pocket-id/backend/internal/model/types"
+	"github.com/pocket-id/pocket-id/backend/internal/model/types"
+	"github.com/pocket-id/pocket-id/backend/internal/oidc"
 	"github.com/pocket-id/pocket-id/backend/internal/storage"
 	"github.com/pocket-id/pocket-id/backend/internal/utils"
 	imageutil "github.com/pocket-id/pocket-id/backend/internal/utils/image"
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the OIDC service by delegating the complex OIDC logic, including audience validation for client credentials, to a new `oidc` package. This change ensures that the audience requested during a client credentials grant is properly validated against the client's allowed audiences, preventing unauthorized token issuance.

## GHSA-447x-qqwf-m7xq · @tsparticles/mcp-server

2026-11-09 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application did not properly handle excessively large HTTP request bodies. Although there was a check for `MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES`, the `req.destroy` call was not wrapped in a `try-catch` block, meaning that if the `req.on("data")` event listener threw an error, the server would crash instead of gracefully handling the oversized request.

### Impact

An attacker could send a large HTTP POST request to the `/mcp` endpoint, causing the server to crash and leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/integrations/mcp-server/src/index.ts
+++ b/integrations/mcp-server/src/index.ts
@@ -398,22 +398,38 @@ async function startHttp(port: number) {
 
     // MCP endpoint — accept POST with JSON body
     if (req.method === "POST" && req.url === "/mcp") {
-      const body = await new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => {
-        const chunks: Buffer[] = [];
-        let receivedBytes = 0;
-        req.on("data", (chunk: Buffer) => {
-          receivedBytes += chunk.length;
-          if (receivedBytes > MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES) {
-            req.destroy(new Error("Request body too large"));
-            return;
-          }
-          chunks.push(chunk);
-        });
-        req.on("end", () => resolve(Buffer.concat(chunks).toString("utf8")));
-        req.on("error", reject);
-      });
+      let body: string;
+
+      try {
+        body = await new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => {
+          const chunks: Buffer[] = [];
+          let receivedBytes = 0;
+          req.on("data", (chunk: Buffer) => {
+            receivedBytes += chunk.length;
+            if (receivedBytes > MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES) {
+              req.destroy(new Error("Request body too large"));
+              return;
+            }
+            chunks.push(chunk);
+          });
+          req.on("end", () => resolve(Buffer.concat(chunks).toString("utf8")));
+          req.on("error", reject);
+        });
+      } catch {
+        res.writeHead(413, { "Content-Type": "application/json" });
+        res.end(JSON.stringify({ error: "Request body too large" }));
+        return;
+      }
+
+      let parsedBody: unknown;
+      try {
+        parsedBody = body ? JSON.parse(body) : undefined;
+      } catch {
+        res.writeHead(400, { "Content-Type": "application/json" });
+        res.end(JSON.stringify({ error: "Invalid JSON" }));
+        return;
+      }
 
-      const parsedBody = body ? JSON.parse(body) : undefined;
       await transport.handleRequest(req, res, parsedBody);
       return;
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch wraps the promise-based body parsing logic in a `try-catch` block. If an error occurs during body reception (e.g., due to exceeding `MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES`), it now sends a 413 'Request body too large' response instead of crashing. It also adds error handling for invalid JSON parsing.

## GHSA-6w3r-6hpr-hp6p · ag-psd

2026-11-09 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `ag-psd` library intended to limit total memory allocation during PSD file decoding using a `totalMemoryLimit` option. However, the `decodeLayerImageData` function incorrectly used a local `memoryLimit` variable derived from `options.memoryLimit` instead of directly using `options.totalMemoryLimit`. This allowed an attacker to bypass the intended memory limit by crafting a PSD file with multiple layers, each consuming memory without decrementing the global `totalMemoryLimit`.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PSD file that, when processed by the library, would consume an uncontrolled amount of memory, leading to a denial of service (DoS) by crashing the application or exhausting system resources.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/psdReader.ts
+++ b/src/psdReader.ts
@@ -636,23 +636,23 @@ export function decodeLayerPixels(layer: Layer, useImageData?: boolean) {
 	decodeLayerImageData(layer, { useImageData });
 }
 
-function decodeLayerImageData(layer: Layer, options: { useImageData?: boolean, throwForMissingFeatures?: boolean, memoryLimit?: number }) {
+function decodeLayerImageData(layer: Layer, options: Pick<ReadOptions, 'useImageData' | 'throwForMissingFeatures' | 'totalMemoryLimit'>) {
 	let { throwForMissingFeatures, useImageData } = options;
 
-	const imageData = getDataFromLayer(layer, LayerDataType.Layer, throwForMissingFeatures, options.memoryLimit);
+	const imageData = getDataFromLayer(layer, LayerDataType.Layer, throwForMissingFeatures, options.totalMemoryLimit);
 	setImageDataOrCanvas(layer, imageData, useImageData);
-	if (options.memoryLimit !== undefined) options.memoryLimit -= imageData?.data.byteLength ?? 0;
+	if (options.totalMemoryLimit !== undefined) options.totalMemoryLimit -= imageData?.data.byteLength ?? 0;
 
 	if (layer.mask) {
-		const maskData = getDataFromLayer(layer, LayerDataType.Mask, throwForMissingFeatures, options.memoryLimit);
+		const maskData = getDataFromLayer(layer, LayerDataType.Mask, throwForMissingFeatures, options.totalMemoryLimit);
 		setImageDataOrCanvas(layer.mask, maskData, useImageData);
-		if (options.memoryLimit !== undefined) options.memoryLimit -= maskData?.data.byteLength ?? 0;
+		if (options.totalMemoryLimit !== undefined) options.totalMemoryLimit -= maskData?.data.byteLength ?? 0;
 	}
 
 	if (layer.realMask) {
-		const maskData = getDataFromLayer(layer, LayerDataType.RealMask, throwForMissingFeatures, options.memoryLimit);
+		const maskData = getDataFromLayer(layer, LayerDataType.RealMask, throwForMissingFeatures, options.totalMemoryLimit);
 		setImageDataOrCanvas(layer.realMask, maskData, useImageData);
-		if (options.memoryLimit !== undefined) options.memoryLimit -= maskData?.data.byteLength ?? 0;
+		if (options.totalMemoryLimit !== undefined) options.totalMemoryLimit -= maskData?.data.byteLength ?? 0;
 	}
 
 	delete layer.rawData;
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `decodeLayerImageData` function to consistently use `options.totalMemoryLimit` instead of a local `memoryLimit` variable. This ensures that memory consumed by each layer and mask is correctly accounted for and decremented from the global `totalMemoryLimit`, preventing uncontrolled memory allocation.

## GHSA-g3c2-rjq2-c763 · github.com/esm-dev/esm.sh/web

2026-11-09 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application used `r.URL.Path` directly in `filepath.Join` without proper sanitization or validation. This allowed an attacker to use encoded dot-segments (e.g., `%2e%2e%2f`) in the URL to traverse directories outside the intended `AppDir`.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary files on the server's file system that the application had permissions to access, potentially leading to sensitive information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/web/handler.go
+++ b/web/handler.go
@@ -61,6 +61,11 @@ func NewHandler(config Config) *Handler {
 
 func (s *Handler) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
 	pathname := r.URL.Path
+	localPath := filepath.FromSlash(strings.TrimPrefix(pathname, "/"))
+	if strings.IndexByte(localPath, 0) >= 0 || (localPath != "" && !filepath.IsLocal(localPath)) {
+		http.Error(w, "Bad Request", http.StatusBadRequest)
+		return
+	}
 	switch pathname {
 	case "/@hmr", "/@refresh", "/@prefresh", "/@vdr":
 		s.ServeInternalJS(w, r, pathname[2:])
@@ -71,7 +76,7 @@ func (s *Handler) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
 		}
 		s.ServeHmrWS(w, r)
 	default:
-		filename := filepath.Join(s.config.AppDir, pathname)
+		filename := filepath.Join(s.config.AppDir, localPath)
 		fi, err := os.Lstat(filename)
 		if err == nil && fi.IsDir() {
 			if pathname != "/" && !strings.HasSuffix(pathname, "/") {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces validation to check if the `localPath` derived from the URL contains null bytes or attempts to escape the local directory using `filepath.IsLocal`. It also ensures that the validated `localPath` is used when joining with `s.config.AppDir`.

## GHSA-h89v-g948-47vp · moleculer

2026-11-09 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the TCP packet parser did not properly validate the 'length' field in incoming packets. An attacker could send a packet with a negative or excessively small 'length' value, which would cause the parser to enter an infinite loop when attempting to slice the packet buffer.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could send a specially crafted TCP packet to a Moleculer service, causing the service to consume 100% CPU and become unresponsive, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/transporters/tcp/parser.js
+++ b/src/transporters/tcp/parser.js
@@ -58,6 +61,14 @@ class Parser extends Writable {
 
 			const length = packet.readInt32BE(1);
 
+			// Guard against malformed length fields. A valid packet is always at
+			// least HEADER_SIZE bytes long. A negative value (the length is read as
+			// a signed int32) or a value smaller than the header would make the
+			// `packet.slice(length)` below a no-op, causing an infinite loop.
+			if (length < HEADER_SIZE) {
+				return cb(new Error("Invalid packet length! " + length));
+			}
+
 			// The chunk contain a message
 			if (packet.length >= length) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check to validate the 'length' field read from the packet header. It now ensures that the 'length' is not negative and is at least the size of the packet header (6 bytes). If an invalid length is detected, an error is thrown, preventing the infinite loop.

## GHSA-vvr2-h2jp-838m · code-graph-rag

2026-11-09 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application constructed file paths by directly concatenating a user-controlled `file_path` with the `project_root`. Without proper sanitization or canonicalization and validation, an attacker could use '..' sequences in `file_path` to escape the intended `project_root` directory.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary files on the server's file system, potentially including sensitive configuration files, source code, or user data, by manipulating the `file_path` parameter.

### Key diff

```
full_path = Path(self.project_root) / file_path
+                project_root = Path(self.project_root).resolve()
+                try:
+                    full_path = (project_root / file_path).resolve()
+                    full_path.relative_to(project_root)
```

### Fix summary

The patch now resolves both the `project_root` and the constructed `full_path` to their canonical forms. It then explicitly checks if the `full_path` is still a sub-path of the `project_root` using `relative_to()`. If the path escapes the root, an error is returned, preventing unauthorized file access.

## GHSA-8cfq-jgf7-8m27 · github.com/traPtitech/traQ

2026-11-09 · Go · **HIGH** 7.3 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the OAuth authorization endpoint did not correctly handle cases where no scopes were explicitly requested (i.e., `req.Scopes` was empty). In such scenarios, the system would incorrectly assume that no scopes were required, allowing an attacker to bypass the scope consent prompt and potentially gain access to resources without proper authorization if a previous token existed for the client.

### Impact

An attacker could escalate their privileges by requesting an OAuth token with `prompt=none` and an empty scope list. If a previous token existed for the client, the system would grant access without re-prompting the user for consent, effectively allowing the attacker to obtain a token with all previously granted scopes without explicit user approval for the current request.

### Key diff

```
--- a/router/oauth2/authorization_endpoint.go
+++ b/router/oauth2/authorization_endpoint.go
@@ -203,10 +203,14 @@ func (h *Handler) AuthorizationEndpointHandler(c *echo.Context) error {
 			return c.Redirect(http.StatusFound, redirectURI.String())
 		}
 		ok := false
+		requiredScopes := req.Scopes
+		if len(requiredScopes) == 0 {
+			requiredScopes = req.ValidScopes
+		}
 		for _, v := range tokens {
 			if v.ClientID == req.ClientID {
 				all := true
-				for s := range req.Scopes {
+				for s := range requiredScopes {
 					if !v.Scopes.Contains(s) {
 						all = false
 						break
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by ensuring that if `req.Scopes` is empty, the system defaults to using `req.ValidScopes` for scope validation. This prevents the system from incorrectly assuming no scopes are required and ensures that existing tokens are checked against all valid scopes, not just an empty set, before granting access without a prompt.

## GHSA-p74g-f95m-7fx7 · github.com/traPtitech/traQ

2026-11-09 · Go · **HIGH** 7.3 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Content-Disposition header for file downloads was conditionally set based on a query parameter. When the 'dl' query parameter was not present or false, the header was not explicitly set to 'attachment', allowing the browser to potentially render the file inline. If the file was an HTML file with malicious content, this could lead to XSS.

### Impact

An attacker could upload a malicious HTML file and then craft a URL that, when visited by another user, would execute arbitrary JavaScript in the victim's browser, leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement.

### Key diff

```
-	if v, _ := strconv.ParseBool(c.QueryParam("dl")); v {
-		c.Response().Header().Set(echo.HeaderContentDisposition, fmt.Sprintf("attachment; filename*=UTF-8''%s", url.PathEscape(meta.GetFileName())))
-	}
+	c.Response().Header().Set(echo.HeaderContentDisposition, attachmentContentDisposition(meta.GetFileName()))
```

### Fix summary

The patch ensures that the Content-Disposition header is always set to 'attachment' for all file downloads, regardless of the 'dl' query parameter. This forces browsers to download the file instead of rendering it inline, mitigating the XSS risk.

## GHSA-85gg-2gfq-q95m · code-graph-rag

2026-11-09 · Python · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application did not properly resolve symbolic links before checking if a path was contained within the project root. This allowed an attacker to create a symlink pointing outside the intended directory, which would then be followed by the application.

### Impact

An attacker could read or potentially overwrite arbitrary files outside the project's intended scope, leading to information disclosure or unauthorized modification of data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/codebase_rag/utils/path_utils.py
+++ b/codebase_rag/utils/path_utils.py
@@ -120,6 +120,14 @@ def should_skip_path(
     _is_file = path.is_file() if is_file is None else is_file
     if _is_file and path.suffix in cs.IGNORE_SUFFIXES:
         return True
+    try:
+        path.resolve().relative_to(repo_path.resolve())
+    except ValueError:
+        return True
     rel_path = cached_relative_path(path, repo_path)
     rel_path_str = rel_path.as_posix()
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a step to resolve the canonical path of both the target path and the repository root using `path.resolve()` before checking for containment. If the resolved path cannot be made relative to the resolved repository path, it indicates an escape and the path is skipped.

## GHSA-g3qx-frcg-j979 · github.com/kubeflow/pipelines

2026-11-09 · Go · **MODERATE** 6.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application was reflecting user-controlled error messages directly into the HTTP response without proper sanitization. Specifically, the `util.Wrap` function was used to include error details, which could contain malicious input, into the response sent to the client.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary client-side scripts into the web page, leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites if a user clicks a specially crafted link.

### Key diff

```
- s.writeErrorToResponse(w, http.StatusBadRequest, util.Wrap(err, "Failed to read pipeline from file"))
+ glog.Errorf("Failed to read pipeline from file: %v", err)
+ s.writeErrorToResponse(w, http.StatusBadRequest, errors.New("Failed to read pipeline from file"))
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the `util.Wrap` function calls with generic error messages (e.g., `errors.New("Failed to read pipeline from file")`) and logs the detailed error internally using `glog.Errorf`. This prevents user-controlled error details from being reflected in the HTTP response.

## GHSA-4p8p-pjpq-2w7h · @tsparticles/mcp-server

2026-11-09 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application did not properly handle malformed JSON requests. Specifically, if the `JSON.parse` function encountered invalid JSON, it would throw an uncaught exception, leading to the termination of the HTTP server process.

### Impact

An attacker could send a specially crafted malformed JSON request to the `/mcp` endpoint, causing the HTTP server process to crash and resulting in a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
-      const parsedBody = body ? JSON.parse(body) : undefined;
+      try {
+        parsedBody = body ? JSON.parse(body) : undefined;
+      } catch {
+        res.writeHead(400, { "Content-Type": "application/json" });
+        res.end(JSON.stringify({ error: "Invalid JSON" }));
+        return;
+      }
```

### Fix summary

The patch wraps the `JSON.parse` call in a `try-catch` block. If `JSON.parse` fails, it now catches the error, sends a 400 Bad Request response with an 'Invalid JSON' error message, and prevents the server from crashing.

## GHSA-6xmv-cjr5-w3q8 · fastapi-code-generator

2026-11-09 · Python · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The `fastapi-code-generator` library directly used unsanitized keys from the `responses` dictionary in OpenAPI specifications to generate Python code. Specifically, the status-code keys were not properly escaped before being embedded into `@app.get` decorators, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary Python code.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious OpenAPI specification that, when processed by `fastapi-code-generator`, would lead to arbitrary code execution on the machine generating the FastAPI application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/fastapi_code_generator/parser.py
+++ b/fastapi_code_generator/parser.py
@@ -211,6 +261,18 @@ class Operation(CachedPropertyModel):
     callbacks: Dict[UsefulStr, List["Operation"]] = {}
     arguments_list: List[Argument] = []
 
+    def model_post_init(self, __context: Any) -> None:
+        self.path = PythonEscapedString.single_quoted(self.path)
+        if self.summary is not None:
+            self.summary = PythonEscapedString.triple_double_quoted(self.summary)
+        if self.additional_responses:
+            self.additional_responses = {
+                PythonEscapedString.single_quoted(status_code): models
+                for status_code, models in self.additional_responses.items()
+            }
+        if self.tags:
+            self.tags = [PythonEscapedString.single_quoted(tag) for tag in self.tags]
+
     @classmethod
     def merge_arguments_with_union(cls, arguments: List[Argument]) -> List[Argument]:
         grouped_arguments: DefaultDict[str, List[Argument]] = DefaultDict(list)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `PythonEscapedString` and helper functions (`_escape_single_quoted_python_string`, `_escape_triple_double_quoted_python_string`) to properly escape string values before they are used in code generation. It applies this escaping to `path`, `summary`, `additional_responses` keys, and `tags` within the `Operation` class, preventing injection.

## GHSA-f42p-wjw5-97qh · zizmor

2026-11-09 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→LOG_EXPOSURE`

### Root cause

The `GitHubToken` struct, which holds sensitive GitHub access tokens, was derived with `Debug`. This automatically generated a `Debug` implementation that printed the token's raw string value when debug logging was enabled, leading to its exposure in logs.

### Impact

An attacker with access to debug logs could retrieve GitHub access tokens, potentially leading to unauthorized access to GitHub resources or accounts associated with the compromised tokens.

### Key diff

```
-#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
+#[derive(Clone)]
 pub(crate) struct GitHubToken(String);
 
 impl GitHubToken {
@@ -110,7 +119,16 @@ impl GitHubToken {
     }
 
     fn to_header_value(&self) -> Result<HeaderValue, InvalidHeaderValue> {
-        HeaderValue::from_str(&format!("Bearer {}", self.0))
+        let mut hv = HeaderValue::from_str(&format!("Bearer {}", self.0))?;
+        hv.set_sensitive(true);
+
+        Ok(hv)
+    }
+}
+
+impl std::fmt::Debug for GitHubToken {
+    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
+        f.debug_tuple("GitHubToken").field(&"***").finish()
     }
 }
```

### Fix summary

The `Debug` derive macro was removed from the `GitHubToken` struct. A custom `Debug` implementation was added to redact the token's value, replacing it with `"***"` in debug output. Additionally, the `set_sensitive(true)` method was called on the `HeaderValue` to ensure it's also redacted in debug contexts.

## GHSA-ghgh-57qv-h687 · github.com/kubeflow/pipelines

2026-11-09 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the authorization check for shared pipelines incorrectly skipped all authentication and authorization for operations on resources with an empty namespace. While this was intended for read-only access, it inadvertently allowed write operations (update, delete) on shared pipelines without proper authorization.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could bypass RBAC rules and delete or modify any global (shared) pipeline, leading to denial of service or unauthorized changes to critical workflows.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/src/apiserver/server/pipeline_server.go
+++ b/backend/src/apiserver/server/pipeline_server.go
@@ -1262,8 +1262,21 @@ func (s *BasePipelineServer) canAccessPipeline(ctx context.Context, pipelineId s
 	// Skip authentication if the namespace is empty to enable shared pipelines in multi-user mode
 	if s.resourceManager.IsEmptyNamespace(resourceAttributes.Namespace) {
+		if resourceAttributes.Verb == common.RbacResourceVerbGet || resourceAttributes.Verb == common.RbacResourceVerbList {
+			return nil
+		}
+		resourceAttributes.Namespace = common.GetPodNamespace()
+		err := s.resourceManager.IsAuthorized(ctx, resourceAttributes)
+		if err != nil {
+			return util.Wrapf(err, "Failed to access shared pipeline %s. Check if you have permission to %s shared pipelines", pipelineId, resourceAttributes.Verb)
+		}
 		return nil
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `canAccessPipeline` function to explicitly differentiate between read-only and write operations on shared pipelines. Read-only operations still bypass authorization, but write operations now correctly enforce authorization by checking permissions against the KFP system namespace.

## GHSA-h667-4jj2-cgv4 · fastapi-code-generator

2026-11-09 · Python · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `fastapi-code-generator` library generated Python code based on OpenAPI specifications. Specifically, the `summary` field from the OpenAPI spec was directly inserted into the generated Python route-handler docstring without proper escaping. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary Python code into the docstring, which would then be executed when the generated code was parsed or run.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the system generating the FastAPI application by crafting a malicious OpenAPI specification. This could lead to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/fastapi_code_generator/parser.py
+++ b/fastapi_code_generator/parser.py
@@ -211,6 +261,18 @@ class Operation(CachedPropertyModel):
     callbacks: Dict[UsefulStr, List["Operation"]] = {}
     arguments_list: List[Argument] = []
 
+    def model_post_init(self, __context: Any) -> None:
+        self.path = PythonEscapedString.single_quoted(self.path)
+        if self.summary is not None:
+            self.summary = PythonEscapedString.triple_double_quoted(self.summary)
+        if self.additional_responses:
+            self.additional_responses = {
+                PythonEscapedString.single_quoted(status_code): models
+                for status_code, models in self.additional_responses.items()
+            }
+        if self.tags:
+            self.tags = [PythonEscapedString.single_quoted(tag) for tag in self.tags]
 
     @classmethod
     def merge_arguments_with_union(cls, arguments: List[Argument]) -> List[Argument]:
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `PythonEscapedString` and helper functions (`_escape_single_quoted_python_string`, `_escape_triple_double_quoted_python_string`) to properly escape string values before they are inserted into the generated Python code. The `Operation` model's `model_post_init` method now uses these new classes to ensure that `path`, `summary`, `additional_responses`, and `tags` are all correctly escaped.

## GHSA-p2mm-64f6-gmpp · fastapi-code-generator

2026-11-09 · Python · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `fastapi-code-generator` library generated Python code by directly embedding user-controlled OpenAPI path strings into `@app.get()` decorators without proper escaping. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary Python code into the generated application by crafting a malicious OpenAPI specification, leading to import-time RCE.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the system running the generated FastAPI application, potentially leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/fastapi_code_generator/parser.py
+++ b/fastapi_code_generator/parser.py
@@ -211,6 +261,18 @@ class Operation(CachedPropertyModel):
     callbacks: Dict[UsefulStr, List["Operation"]] = {}
     arguments_list: List[Argument] = []
 
+    def model_post_init(self, __context: Any) -> None:
+        self.path = PythonEscapedString.single_quoted(self.path)
+        if self.summary is not None:
+            self.summary = PythonEscapedString.triple_double_quoted(self.summary)
+        if self.additional_responses:
+            self.additional_responses = {
+                PythonEscapedString.single_quoted(status_code): models
+                for status_code, models in self.additional_responses.items()
+            }
+        if self.tags:
+            self.tags = [PythonEscapedString.single_quoted(tag) for tag in self.tags]
+
     @classmethod
     def merge_arguments_with_union(cls, arguments: List[Argument]) -> List[Argument]:
         grouped_arguments: DefaultDict[str, List[Argument]] = DefaultDict(list)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `PythonEscapedString` class and helper functions (`_escape_single_quoted_python_string`, `_escape_triple_double_quoted_python_string`) to properly escape user-controlled strings before they are embedded into the generated Python code. Specifically, the `Operation.path` attribute and other relevant fields are now wrapped with `PythonEscapedString.single_quoted` to ensure they are safely represented as string literals.
